I have the honour to present the position of the club Law and Justice in relation to the draft law amending the Law on healthcare services financed by public funds and the Law - Pharmaceutical Law. The aim of this project is to bring the provisions into line with the legal status of the Law on public-funded benefits into line with the legal status of the United Kingdom of Great Britain and Northern Ireland from the European Union, as well as the need to adapt the provisions of the Agreement on the withdrawal of the United Kingdom from the European Union. Accordingly, provision should be made to ensure that health benefits are provided to UK citizens who reside in our country. This is therefore a alignment of the provisions with the withdrawal agreement. In addition, as the rapporteur has put it, the law contains changes in pharmaceutical law, among others. gives the possibility to allow medicinal products, including vaccines in packs with English-language description in situations where there is a gross lack of this medicinal product in the Republic of Poland. The amendments referred to by the rapporteur were also tabled, namely amendments to the law on the prevention and control of communicable diseases in humans, where an additional provision was introduced that medical rescuers could carry out protective vaccinations, and another provision was added on the basis of which nurses, midwives, as well as pharmacists could qualify for vaccination against adult influenza, i.e. such an extension of the competences of those persons. This project, of course, contains changes in the financing of health care, as presented at the committee meeting. Spa treatment, unfortunately for epidemic reasons, could not carry out its tasks, there was a significant reduction in revenue, in many cases it led to a loss of liquidity. It was therefore decided to change the financing of health care benefits during the pandemic period. On this basis, these entities will be able to obtain additional funding of 70% It's a monthly rate. Thus, these rules go far against both the public and the healthcare providers who provide medical services in this area. The project is very good, positively rated by the club Law and Justice. The Law and Justice Club recommends that the High House adopt this proposal. However, we would like to make three further amendments at this stage of the procedure, namely the 1st amendment concerns the Law on medicinal activities. In this law, we would like to make a change regarding the return of the exit allowance previously granted on the basis of the no longer applicable law on health care facilities for workers employed in medical rescue teams. The law that I mentioned, which no longer applies, gave the possibility to grant 30 percent of the rescue teams an allowance. This law has ceased to apply and rescuers have lost their ability to obtain these funds. Therefore, we would like this amendment to amend the Act on Medical Activity and this supplement to be restored. In addition, we also propose amendments to the Law on specific arrangements for the prevention and eradication of infectious diseases. This change refers to the clarification of the financing of these spa benefits in different types of spa benefits. Therefore, this requires a revision of the bill. That's the amendment we'd like to make. And yet another amendment, Amendment 3, which is directly related to Amendment 1, namely, we propose that the 30% supplement I was talking about in the Medical Activity Act should enter into force 14 days after the date of the announcement, and I apologise for the fact that this supplement has entered into force since 1 October 2021. I will answer the questions of individual Members one at a time. Mr Szopiński asked why it was in the rank of law for the citizens of Great Britain and Northern Ireland. We're bound by treaties. We are in the European Union and all rights relating to benefits in kind are governed by the Treaty on European Union in respect of countries that have been associated. Therefore, if the United Kingdom with Northern Ireland leaves, we need to define it by law. All the provisions that apply to the European Union are simply added to the UK too, so that there are no problems with physical security on the same basis as is the case with citizens of the European Union. Since the National Health Fund is, at the same time, a liaison body responsible for international accounts, for settlements with other countries, the United Kingdom and Ireland will return to these countries. These settlements are carried out on an ongoing basis. No one can see it at all, but every patient is accounted for when it comes to the performance of benefits. As in Poland, people are treated in a planned manner with the consent of their insurers. Also in a sudden manner, the performance of these benefits is always carried out and the providers receive remuneration for the performance of the benefits. As regards the question of Mr Rafał Adamczyk, why leaflets in Polish are not possible in the case of vaccines, as a general rule, as regards hospital medicine, the President of the Office for the Registration of Medicinal Products, Medical Products and Biocidal Products, has the right to issue such consent. There is no such right only with regard to outpatient treatment. Vaccines are known to be administered primarily in outpatient treatment. Therefore, according to today's law, this cannot be authorised, and the main issue concerns vaccines, not with regard to population vaccinations, as this amendment does not apply to population vaccinations, i.e. vaccinations from the preventive vaccination and vaccination programmes recommended, but to the others. These are exit vaccines, special vaccines, which are often used in small quantities - less than 1000. Therefore, the preparation of the machine for the production of Polish leaflets, Polish packaging is too expensive and would make it impossible to allow these vaccines on the Polish market. Of course, the question was whether we were concerned that it would somehow strengthen the anti-vaccine movement. Of course not, as I said, these are unique vaccines and if we do not do so, the companies have provided us with a calculation that they will have problems with the introduction of drugs, vaccines to the Polish market. So it's about the safety of vaccination. But as I say, this does not apply to preventive vaccination, so we are not afraid of this phenomenon in this regard. What's the case: COVID a flu. I know that now medicinal products are getting ready for the third dose that they're going to vaccinate, for example. at the same time as the third dose and flu vaccine, as influenza vaccines have been secured by the State for each of the eligible groups. These groups are quite wide, it takes place outside the usual distribution channel, which has been so far, i.e. outside pharmacies and outside employers, who bought health care services in the form of vaccinations. In addition, the Government's Strategic Reserve Agency gives vaccines free of charge to authorised entities, i.e. staff employed in medicinal entities, pharmacies, long-term care patients and social services homes, all patients over 75 years of age, as well as teachers, school educators. So that these people are entitled to vaccinate from a pool of vaccines purchased by the Government's Strategic Reserve Agency and this channel is already running. ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-13-sejm-39-1.u69	Thank you very much. High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, I present a report on a government draft law amending the Waste Act and some other laws. The project entered the Sejm on 15 September 2021 and on 21 September was aimed at first reading in the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry. On 30 September, after the first reading, the committee decided that detailed work on the project would be carried out in the Permanent Sub-Committee on the Monitoring of Waste Management. This took place on 6 October, while the report of the Environment Committee was adopted yesterday - 12 October - 26 votes in favour, with no votes against and with one abstention. High Izbo! This draft law implements four Directives of the European Union: Directive 94/62/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council of 20 December 1994 on packaging and packaging waste, Council Directive 1999/31/EC of 26 April 1999 on the landfill of waste, Directive 2008/98/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council of 19 November 2008 on waste and repealing certain Directives and Directive 2012/19/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 4 July 2012 on waste electrical and electronic equipment. All these Directives were amended in 2018 by Directives published in the Official Journal of the European Union of 14 June 2018. The revision of EU legislation on waste management concerns clarification of the rules implementing the circular economy. The targets for preparing for the re-use and recycling of municipal waste by 2025, 2030 or 2035 are set. Targets are set for recycling packaging waste by 2025 and 2030 and for reducing municipal waste disposal by 2035. In order to amend these Directives, the proposal amends seven laws. Art. 1 contains 44 amendments to the Waste Act of 14 December 2012. An exemption from the provisions of this Law is added for feed materials which do not contain animal by-products and for waste management from land-based mining, exploration, extraction, processing and storage of mineral raw materials. The definitions of biowaste, waste management and waste prevention and municipal waste shall be amended. The definitions of non-dangerous waste, construction and demolition waste, food waste, extended producer responsibility system, material recovery, earthwork and excavation shall be added. A new Chapter 2a is inserted, entitled "Prevention of waste', where in the following article. 19a are calculated in an open catalogue of methods of prevention such as promoting and supporting sustainable production and consumption models, encouraging the design, manufacture and use of resource-efficient, sustainable, repairable, reuse and modernisation of products. Another action referred to in Article. 19a This is to support the availability of spare parts, operating instructions, technical information, equipment or software enabling the repair and reuse of products, reducing the generation of waste in industry, excavating minerals, construction, demolition and manufacturing, reducing the production of food waste, promoting food donations and other forms of redistribution, and to develop and support awareness raising information campaigns. High Izbo! In view of the requirements of Directive 2008/98/EC, not only the provisions on waste prevention but also the scope of waste management plans are extended. The Member States of the European Union have an obligation to develop plans to prevent food waste, as well as waste prevention programmes, which should cover very different areas of economic activity, production processes or services. The actions set out in the Waste Prevention Programme will be designed on the basis of data and information provided annually by individual ministries to the Minister for Climate and Environment. The scope and form of these data can be defined by the Minister for Climate and Environment by a Regulation. In this way, monitoring of the implementation of the tasks set out in the Task Plan and reporting to the European Commission will be carried out. The National Waste Management Plan and the Voivodship Waste Management Plans will have to take into account solutions for waste containing significant quantities of critical raw materials, which are difficult to obtain, due to complex and difficult environmental measures. In addition, the Voivodship Waste Management Plan will include assessments of existing waste collection systems, including selective waste collection, as well as information on any derogations granted in accordance with European Union legislation. So much for changing the waste bill. Art. 2 contains 14 amendments to the Law of 13 September 1996 on the maintenance of cleanliness and order in municipalities. The overarching objective of the regulation is to reduce the quantity of waste stored to 10% by 2035. , in accordance with the provision of Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 5 Directive 1999/31/EC on the landfill of waste. Amendment 3 to Article. 3b Cleanliness and order law in municipalities shall set intermediate targets for these levels of storage: 30% by weight per year between 2025 and 2029, 20% by weight per year between 2030 and 2034 and 10% by weight per year in 2035 and subsequent years. These records are complementary because the system already in force sets the requirement to collect municipal waste in a selective and higher manner for storage, in particular for the storage of unprocessed waste. The statutory records required from recycling municipalities and other recovery processes also operate in this way. The point is that the maximum amount of waste to be recovered is to collect selectively. Then, of course, what remains to be stored decreases. The definition of these levels is clear and requires very complex and systematic actions carried out by municipalities. In summary, the mere imposition of limits is not sufficient, it is necessary to increase the level of recycling, treatment of waste in order to achieve a reduction in the amount of waste stored. As the definitions of construction and demolition waste, which were included in the responsibilities of the municipality, were introduced, the responsibilities of the municipality to receive and report on construction and demolition waste arising in households were clarified. Art. 3 concerns the Act of 24 April 2009 on batteries and accumulators and introduces changes to reporting by means of a waste database within the scope of this Act. Art. 4 amends the Law of 25 February 2011 on chemicals and mixtures thereof. Added article. 25a shall impose on suppliers of the products referred to in Article. Point 33 of Regulation (EC) No 1907/2006 of the European Parliament and of the Council requires the transmission of the obligation required by Article. 33 of that Regulation, relevant information to the European Chemicals Agency. In turn, the following article is added: 54a contains a provision concerning penalties imposed for failure to provide the above mentioned. the information required by Article. Article 9 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 waste directive. So much for the changes in this bill. Art. 5 contains 23 amendments to the Act of 13 June 2013 on the management of packaging and packaging waste. In view of the amendments to Directive 94/62/EC, the definitions of packaging waste, re-use packaging and multi-material packaging shall be amended. Recyclable levels in accordance with Article. 6 Directive 94/62/EC will be transposed into Polish law, as has been the case so far, by amendments to the Package Management and Packaging Waste Act, i.e. Article 2 20 and Annex 1 and the annual gradual increase in recycling levels in order to reach the levels set out in the Directive for 2025 and 2030 will be set out in the Regulation. In addition, the rules specify how to measure the mass of recycled waste. This will occur during the introduction of waste into recycling processes, after having been subjected to all necessary controls, sorting and other preliminary operations, as well as using the tools provided by the waste database. The idea is to include in the statistics only the waste that will actually be recycled. References to recovery, including DPO and EDPO documents, will be deleted from the Act as only recycling levels have been identified in the revised Directive. Therefore, only recycling levels will be specified in the Package Management and Packaging Waste Act, excluding recovery other than recycling. So much for changes to the Package Management Act. Art. 6 of the project concerns the Act of 11 September 2015 on waste electrical and electronic equipment. It contains provisions specifying how to determine the weight of recycled waste. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 7 is amended by the Law of 4 July 2019 amending the Waste Act and certain other laws. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 8 the Law of 19 July 2019 amending the Law on Cleanliness and Order in municipalities is amended. Art. The measures provided for in this Regulation are in accordance with the opinion of the Standing Committee on Plants, Art. 19 provides that the Act shall enter into force on 1 January 2022, with the exception of amendments 35 and 40 to Article. 1 which shall enter into force on 1 January 2023. Here I would like to draw your attention to the fact that, during the work of the subcommittee, 18 legislative amendments and three substantive amendments were introduced to the Act. The last of these substantive amendments concerned only Article. 14 and Article 14 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles In the Government's submission, the deadline for entry into force was 30 days, and now the deadline is longer. Therefore, derogations from the main date of entry into force of the Act have decreased. These are only changes 35th and 40th in Article. 1 which, as I said, enter into force on 1 January 2023. All other provisions of the Act are in effect from 1 January next year. I will yet draw attention to the last amendment that was introduced at the stage of the committee's report. It's about art. Article 9 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 and provides that the national waste prevention programme referred to in Article 1 shall be drawn up for the first time. paragraph 34a. 1 Act as amended by Art. 1, i.e. the Waste Act, does not apply the provisions of Article. 36a of this bill. The point is that the national waste management plan adopted by the Resolution of the Council of Ministers of July 2016 is currently in force. Due to the need to update in 6-year mode, it should be revised by June next year. Since we have delayed the date of entry into force of the Act by almost 3 months, the procedure laid down in the article will not be maintained. 36a act, but the situation is beneficial for all obliged entities, because vacathio legis is longer. ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-13-sejm-39-1.u117	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I have the honour to present the position of the Council of Ministers vis-à-vis the draft law amending the Act on Electromobility and Alternative Fuels and some other laws, Parliamentary Printing No. 1633. The world transport market is changing dynamically. We are facing a revolution that will change our thinking about the means of transport and the fuels used in this sector. We will increasingly use low-carbon alternative fuels, including electricity and hydrogen. As a result, we'll breathe better air. We will reduce the number of respiratory diseases, cardiovascular diseases and put a brick to stop climate change. We will achieve this by reducing emissions of harmful substances emitted from motor vehicles such as oxides nitrogen NO3, particulates, particulates suspended and reduction of carbon dioxide emissions. The development of electromobility is also beneficial for the development of innovative industry. It is worth noting that the Polish position in the European Union, when it comes to the export of electric buses, has clearly strengthened. Global exports increased from 10% in 2017 to close to 50% in 2020. Thus Poland became the largest exporter of electric buses in the European Union. We have ahead of Belgium, which accounts for 36% of EU exports in this sector. The largest producer of electric buses in Poland is Solaris, which has so far won orders for more than 1,000. electric vehicles. According to data from the Central Statistical Office, in 2020 Polish producers delivered electric buses worth ca. 220 million euro, which means an increase of more than 50% year-to-year. Poland also has a chance to become a European hub when it comes to producing batteries for electric cars. The demand for batteries in Europe will rise rapidly in the coming years. Market analysts estimate that in 2025 it will be about 300 GWh, in 2030 it will be 700 GWh and in 2035 it will be over 1300 GWh. Several major Korean companies have decided to place their factories in our country. I may not mention their names, but, ladies and gentlemen, these are projects with a total value of over PLN 7 billion. It is worth mentioning the LG Chem factory near Wroclaw, which currently employs more than 10 thousand. persons. Once the target production capacity of 100 GWh is reached in the near future, it will be one of the largest plants producing batteries for electric cars in the world. The Polish government, the government of Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki, creates the best conditions for investing in our country, among others. for industries in the field of electromobility, electric buses, but also the battery industry. Thanks to these investments in 2020 Poland was the largest exporter of lithium-ion batteries from European Union countries. The value of exports amounted to nearly EUR 4 billion, which represented 1.7% the total value of Polish exports. Compared to 2019 exports increased by almost EUR 2 billion, which was the biggest annual increase in foreign sales of a single position in the statistics of Polish foreign trade. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Act on Electromobility and Alternative Fuels has been in force in Poland since 2018, which regulates the functioning of the market and sets objectives to be achieved. Due to the continuous development of electromobility in Poland and in the world, it is necessary to modify and supplement existing regulations. The aim of the change is to create the best possible regulation for the development of electromobility - both for citizens themselves and for markets, for entrepreneurs who are developing this industry. Therefore, the draft amendment to the Act on Electromobility and Alternative Fuels, which I have the honour to present to the High House, includes a revision of the rules on the establishment of clean transport zones, the possibility of creating them in all municipalities and the possibility of establishing individual entry rights. The zones will be created by a resolution of the municipality council which will determine the rules for their functioning. Thus, the decision to establish a clean transport zone, as well as to define the rules for its operation, its use, as well as the exemptions of individual persons, entities, institutions for the use of the zone shall be taken by the municipal council in accordance with the resolution adopted. We propose new solutions on the depreciation limit for combustion and low-carbon vehicles, introducing a fixed depreciation rate for hydrogen powered vehicles of 225 000. zł - currently the limit for electric vehicles also amounts to 225 thousand. zł - further reduce the depreciation limit for vehicles emitting more than 50 g/km CO2 from 1 January 2026 from the current 150 000. PLN to 100,000. zł. We give, High Izbo, a very long vacathio legis, 5 years, so that everyone who uses vehicles with a conventional engine for business purposes can make a decision, plan to buy a car that is low- or zero-emission. In addition, we propose the introduction of definitions and regulations enabling the development of hydrogen economy, the definition of hydrogen filling stations, the design of regulations governing the operation of hydrogen filling infrastructure, the facilitation of installation in buildings of multi-family charging points for electric vehicles, the implementation of Directive No 1161 of 2019 on the promotion of clean and energy-efficient transport vehicles in Poland. The provisions aim to increase the participation of low- and zero-emission vehicles in tender procedures. We are also implementing Directive No 944 of 2019 on common rules for the internal market in electricity into the Polish legal order. The provisions prohibit the operators of the electricity distribution system of charging stations for vehicles due to their privileged position. This results in the abolition of the intervention mechanism provided for in the Act, according to which the OSD were to build vehicle charging stations in selected cities in Poland. In addition, we implement Directive No 844 of 2018 on the energy performance of buildings into Polish law. These provisions specify the obligation to install charging points and ducts and electrical cables in designed and renovated buildings. This is, of course, about electric vehicle charging systems. The draft amendment was subject to extensive public consultation. Many comments were made by both citizens and public authorities. I can say with a clear conscience that the project presented is balanced in all its demands, trying to reconcile often conflicting interests. I would also like to thank non-governmental organisations, associations bringing together stakeholders in the electromobility industry, but also organisations whose guiding action is concern for quality, clean air in Poland, for excellent cooperation, for many discussions, consultations in official mode, but also in direct mode - meetings in the Ministry of Climate and Environment, which resulted in the excellent wording of this bill. The amendment is another point in the process of implementing a consistent policy of government support for the development of electromobility in Poland. Stable financial revenues have allowed the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management to launch two programmes, the Green Public Transport programme, aimed at local government units, which finances the purchase of electric and hydrogen-powered buses and the infrastructure for charging and refueling these vehicles, and the "My Electric' programme dedicated to both individuals, individual customers and companies, for the institution where the funding for the purchase of electric cars was designed. In January 2021, applications for funding from the priority programme of the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management were selected, with a budget of PLN 1100 million and funds for the purchase of zero-emission rolling stock and infrastructure for charging or refueling these vehicles. In addition, 200 million PLN was made available under this scheme for loans to local government units, low-interest loans, which may complement the financing of a given project. The call was of great interest and was closed, attention, 14 days after its launch due to the exhaustion of funds. This also shows that both the programme and the actions undertaken systematically by the government meet with the approval of local governments, meet with acceptance of the beneficiaries. Of course, we are working on the continuation of this programme, which will be carried out in the future. Currently, the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management has already announced the second phase of this programme. The budget of this phase is PLN 1 billion for grants and PLN 200 million for loans. The selection of applications shall be carried out on a competitive basis from 1 September to 20 December 2021. It is at this moment that Polish municipalities, Polish self-governments in the care of the quality of transport services in their cities, in their municipalities, submit applications to the program "Green public transport." Another program I mentioned is my electrician. It is currently aimed at individuals. Given that entrepreneurs purchase most of the new vehicles in Poland, recruitments will be launched soon also to entrepreneurs. I would like to say that this is not due to any delay or delay on the part of the Ministry or the national fund, but only to the necessary and mandatory notification of the state aid rules for which we are in the final phase of the agreement with the European Commission at the moment. It should be noted that leasing is the main way for entrepreneurs to purchase vehicles, and that is why my electrician programme, in addition to the acquisition support, is currently also providing subsidies to the so-called. leasing charges, which are of great interest and acceptance by entrepreneurs. Funding will also be available for local government units, associations, farmers and religious associations. The budget of the programme is PLN 500 million. Another PLN 800 million under the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management will be allocated to support the development of alternative fuels infrastructure. We are currently looking forward to the European Commission's approval of support, i.e. acceptance of the conditions for state aid. In total, the funds to be allocated to the development of electromobility are PLN 3800 million over the next few years. Finally, I would like to stress that the aim of the proposal to amend the Act on Electromobility and Alternative Fuels is to accelerate the development of electromobility and the entire sector of zero- and low-carbon transport in Poland. The proposed rules are positively assessed by the entrepreneurs themselves operating in this sector, but also, as I mentioned earlier, by business organisations, NGOs bringing together market stakeholders, but also organisations whose main concern and area of activity is action to improve air quality in Poland. Local governments expect legislation on clean transport zones and announce it in their cities. Therefore, our common goal is to proceed as efficiently as possible on this project at the stage of Parliament's work. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I ask you kindly to accept this bill. Thank you very much.

The proposed amendment aims to respond to societal expectations and to adapt to the faster and faster development of the automotive industry and to further support the development of electromobility and alternative fuel infrastructure and thus encourage consumers to buy vehicles powered by these fuels. It is also about adapting the rules in force to those in the climate and environment declared by the Minister for Electromobility. In addition, the draft law on its regulation transposes the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of the European Union into Polish law in the case of broadly understood energy efficiency. In the context of the proposed solutions in the area covered by the above mentioned above. The draft law should be understood to be measures aimed at reducing emissions, particularly in urban centres where it is the highest. This action is justified for health and environmental reasons. The proposed changes are mainly: clarification of the rules on the establishment of clean transport zones, the possibility of establishing them in all municipalities, the definition of individual entry rights, the introduction of facilitations in planning and design of charging points in multi-family buildings, including the definition of the obligation to provide adequate connection capacity for buildings, the introduction of facilitation in obtaining approval for the installation of charging points in new multi-station parking spaces in communities and housing cooperatives, the definition of the powers of the Technical Supervision Authority and the Transport Technical Supervision Authority to carry out research and control of alternative fuel infrastructure, the clarification of issues related to charging stations, including their construction, the revision of the definition of charging stations, the introduction of the obligation to obtain individual identification codes, the recognition of the code obtained in another European Union country and the definition of depreciation rules for low-carbon vehicles. It is particularly worth emphasising the introduction of provisions enabling the development of hydrogen filling infrastructure and, consequently, the development of the hydrogen economy, the introduction of the possibility of providing financial support where an entrepreneur does not buy a vehicle and benefits from leasing, as well as the implementation of European Parliament Directives 2019/1161 and 2019/944 and 2018/844. The proposed changes are also designed to facilitate the approval of the installation of a charging point in multi-station parking spaces in communities or housing cooperatives. It should be clarified in which cases such consent may be refused. Additional rules should require planning for the development of charging stations when designing housing estates. Mrs. Marshal! So it's about gas fuel, compressed fuel, also CNG, LNG, but it's about biomethane, which has a zero balance throughout the production and combustion cycle. Such a CNG or LNG bus from biomethane will benefit from the quality of the zero-emission bus - not even low- but zero-emission bus. On the other hand, when it comes to commercial vehicles, these cars last mile, they can also use gas, this time natural, but compressed, CNG or liquefied LNG. We have excluded support for fuel called LPG as a fuel derived from refinery products and in fact almost 100% imported to Poland, without this quality of development of the Polish market. Of course, we want the fees generated in the use of clean transport zones to be allocated, among others. to support the purchase of electric bicycles. Let us remember that the action in question, in order to be included by the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management in the support programme, must primarily result from the so-called. the environmental impact. If we do not find an ecological effect, it is difficult to justify funding for the purchase of a given means of transport. I do not want to indicate a specific date, but this year - I hope it will be November - I am sure that the call for proposals for funding for the construction of charging infrastructure for electric vehicles will be launched. We want to place the main emphasis on high-speed filling stations with a single output of 150-200 kW or even more. These are stations modeled on large conventional stations. Today, when we arrive at a conventional-powered car station, we see that there are several distributors: 6, 8, sometimes more, and such a model was proposed as an appropriate business model to ensure high capacity when it comes to charging electric vehicles. This will also lead to the development of the market. You will admit that if I come by electric car to charge this vehicle and I have to wait a few hours for the distributor, this charging station or the charging point to be released, I am discouraged. It makes it harder for the market to grow. So I think that the appropriate emphasis placed on the development of large electric vehicle charging stations, with high power and at the same time on the construction of trafostations that will accompany it, with rest points, to drink coffee, to arrange some cases, to pay the bill, of course, will translate into a model whose application can stimulate the development of electromobility in Poland. ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-13-sejm-39-1.u171	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I was honoured to present a report by the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry and the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development on the Senate draft law amending the law - Hunting Law, print No 1400. On 14 July 2021, the Sejm Marshal addressed the draft law to the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry and the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development for first reading. The joint committees of 29 September 2021 discussed the draft law at their joint meeting. No amendments were tabled at first reading. The proposal for a law amending the Law - Hunting Law extends the right to compensation for hunting damages also to cases in which the claimer refused to accept the construction of equipment or to carry out procedures to prevent damage, if these damages were not caused by the refusal of consent. The contested bill adapts the Polish system to the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 8 May 2019. The amended provision of the Article. 48 point 3 of the Hunting Law will give the damage assessment team the obligation to establish a causal link between the lack of agreement between the tenant or the manager of the hunting equipment circuit or the pursuit or the hunting operation and the damage caused. The amendment to the Hunting Law will benefit the owners and holders of agricultural land who refused to permit the construction of equipment or the execution of procedures to prevent hunting damage. They shall be entitled to compensation for hunting damages if such damage is not caused by refusal of consent or arising in the pursuit of hunting. The Committee on the Protection of the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, together with the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development, after first reading and considering this draft law, shall ask the High Sejm to approve the attached draft law contained in print No 1400 without amendment. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-13-sejm-39-1.u173	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The proposed provisions are intended to extend the circle of operators entitled to receive compensation for hunting damage to owners of damaged crops or agricultural foetuses who refused to permit the construction of equipment or to carry out procedures to prevent damage by the tenant or the manager of the hunting circuit, where such damage was not caused by a causal consequence of refusal of consent and where the damage was caused in the pursuit of hunting. The need to adopt the proposed rules is primarily due to the need to implement the Constitutional Tribunal judgment of 8 May 2019. It also raises no greater doubts. In view of the above, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice welcomes the proposal for a law amending the Law - Hunting Law and is in favour of referring it to further proceedings. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-13-sejm-39-1.u175	Mr Kaczyński, Chairman of the Committee on Security, Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On my behalf, but also on behalf of the Civic Coalition Club, I would like to thank the Senate and Senator Adam Szejnfeld first of all for preparing this amendment of the law - Hunting Law from print No 1400. I am very pleased that the Upper House is vigilant and adapts the law so as to comply with the Polish Constitution, and understands the importance of property rights. In the current version of the Act - Hunting Law Art. 48 (3) provides that compensation shall not be granted: owners of damaged crops or agricultural crops who have not agreed to the construction by the tenant or the district manager of hunting equipment or to the performance of treatment to prevent damage. This provision certainly limits the possibility for farmers to claim compensation from tenants or managers of hunting circuits. However, in this amendment, the drafter proposes that this provision be extended to include cases where the damage was caused independently of the refusal granted. In addition, thanks to this project, this provision will apply to all proceedings, even those which have not been initiated or have not been completed before the entry into force of this law. The amendment, although it does not eliminate or reduce the phenomenon of hunting, is a bow towards farmers and upholds their rights. The project does not cover all issues of responsibility for hunting damages, including those relating to the elimination of restrictions on compensation, but is certainly a step in the right direction and, of course, as the Civic Coalition Club we will support this project. However, I think this is a good time to take the opportunity that we are talking about amending the law - Hunting Law, introducing further amendments and working on this law to adapt hunting to reality, to the state of knowledge in the 21st century, and to focus throughout the discussion about hunting not only on hunters, but also on the protection of animals. Let me remind you that only in one hunting season less than 2.5 million wild animals, including 700,000, were killed. birds. Let us change the Law of Hunting so that killing an animal is necessary, inevitable and justified on safety or economic grounds. Let's get all the references to tradition out of this bill. Is killing animals in the 21st century to be considered a Polish tradition? Let's ban the inhumane acquisition of trophies. Finally, let the animal's body be treated with respect, as it is treated in the case of accompanying animals. Let's ban hunting, feeding and harassing animals. Let us introduce permanent possibilities for disabling land from hunting circuits. The right to privacy and ownership is one of the fundamental rights of man and citizen, and the temporary possibility of excluding land from the hunting circuit is an extreme restriction of this right and if we respect this right of property in this amendment, let us be consistent in this and in this respect Let's adjust them too. Let's not allow commercial hunting. Let hunting be a last resort when all other means fail the exam. In conclusion, I am very pleased that we are beginning to amend the law - Hunting Law and, as I said at the beginning, of course, we are in favour of the amendment from print No 1400, because we care about property rights, but I very much encourage the High House to continue to work on this law. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-13-sejm-39-1.u177	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! So far, 110 outbreaks have been reported in the country. Moreover, new outbreaks appear where large-scale, collective wild boars were carried out. Both scientists and natural organizations have said from the beginning that this strategy that the government then proposed is inadequate. Because the most important thing is the implementation and control of the bioinsurance programme aimed at farmers. These must be systemic actions, and these must be actions where farmers actually have government support. This must be passive surveillance, i.e. searching and immediate disposal of the dead due to wild boar virus - here, as we know, very little or almost nothing is done - and thoughtful firing carried out with absolute preservation of the principles of bioinsurance as a last resort. In fact, none of these proposals, which would actually be adequate for the fight against the ASF epidemic, has been implemented. ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-13-sejm-39-1.u225	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club in relation to the committee's report on the draft law amending the Accounting Act and certain other laws, print Nos 1570 and 1655. Ladies and gentlemen! The contested law adapts the existing reporting and auditing provisions to new technological possibilities and to the current economic and legal situation. The amendment concerns the following laws: the Accounting Act, the Corporate Income Tax Act, the National Court Register, statutory auditors, audit firms and public oversight, as well as the so-called. Around-COVID law - on specific support instruments in connection with the spread of SARS-CoV-2 virus. The proposed amendment to the Act on Auditors related to the progressive digitization of economic life contributes to improved communication between the Polish Audit Supervisory Agency and audit firms. Additional regulations for audit firms will include, inter alia, clarification of the situation in which there is no breach of the professional secrecy of the statutory auditor. Moreover, the provisions concerning the carrying out of audits in audit firms by the Polish Audit Supervisory Agency have been clarified remotely using electronic means. In addition, the provisions for the audit report are defined. The attention of the High House deserves the fact that the contested law introduces a number of facilitations in the operation of many actors. For entities with multi-member boards, it is envisaged to facilitate the signing of financial statements. For listed companies, the effect of regulation will be to reduce the burden by allowing reporting obligations to be fulfilled in a single format for different purposes. The Act also provides for remote meetings of the Council of the Polish Audit Supervisory Agency and the National Council of Certified Auditors and the National Audit Committee of the Polish Chamber of Certified Auditors, including voting in a circular manner outside the meeting. Dear Marshal, High Izbo! In conclusion, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I declare further support for this Act. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-13-sejm-39-1.u226	Thank you very much. Mrs Zofia Czernow, Parliamentary Club Civic Coalition.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-13-sejm-39-1.u227	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Coalition Parliamentary Club on the government draft law amending the Accounting Act and some other laws, print Nos 1570 and 1655. The proposed law mainly aims at adapting the current financial reporting and auditing rules to the current legal and economic situation and new technological opportunities. Proposals for changes are based on, among others, the provisions of Commission Delegated Regulation 2019/815 of December 2018, supplementing Directive 2004/109/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council with regard to regulatory technical standards for specifications of a uniform electronic reporting format. The draft law amends mainly the following legal acts: the Accounting Act, the Audit Act, audit firms and public oversight. The dynamic development of digital technologies also has a significant impact on accounting and financial revision. The possibility of using modern IT tools makes it easier for individuals to fulfil their accounting obligations and to draw up financial statements. Under Regulation 2019/815 issuers whose securities are admitted to trading on a regulated market are required to draw up their annual financial statements in a special format. This solution aims to increase the transparency, accessibility and comparability of the financial statements drawn up by listed companies and facilitate the analysis of those accounts. The proposal also facilitates the signing of financial statements, in particular for bodies led by a multi-member body. The obligation to sign reports by all members of a multi-member body to allow at least one member to do so shall be waived. The amendment to the National Court Register Act introduces the possibility of submitting electronic copies of documents, statements, refusals to make a statement and refusals to sign or other declarations to the National Court Register. Numerous amendments are made to the Act on statutory auditors, audit firms and public oversight. In the audit report, the auditor shall express his opinion on the compliance of the audited report with the requirements of Regulation 2019/815 of the European Union Commission. Rules are also introduced for the submission of an application for the modification of the data to be included in the list of audit firms and set out the procedures for its examination by the Agency. In addition, provision is made for the submission of statements of responsibility for false declarations, as well as the possibility of submitting an electronic application for listing or deletion of an audit firm. Part of the regulation relates to the breach of professional secrecy by the statutory auditor and other matters relating to the work of auditors, e.g. forms and conditions of remote meetings - which is related to COVID-19 - including the possibility of carrying out audits of the audit firm in a remote form. The proposed amendments introduce into the Polish legal system regulations of EU law and other amendments, in particular concerning auditors, which need to be clarified. The draft law was subject to public consultation - as a result of which proposals for amendments to the provisions of the law were largely taken into account or explained - including among others. The Polish Chamber of Experts, the Financial Supervision Authority, the Accounting Associations in Poland and other institutions. During the procedure of the bill at the meeting of the Committee on Public Finances, in the presence of representatives of the Professional Self-Government of Experts, no significant objections were raised, and those raised, individual comments, which may possibly cause problems in the application of this law, in particular technical problems, require, in my opinion, an evaluation of the law and possible changes within six months of its implementation. To sum up, the Civic Coalition Club will vote in favour of this project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Amendment of the Act - The law on road traffic is actually composed of four independent components. Firstly, it is a codification of provisions on speed limiters for vehicles, secondly, a narrowing of the catalogue of vehicles subject to the reporting of vehicle replacement control stations by road meters for reading, thirdly, the introduction of the same rules for the payment of tolls for the issue of non-normative vehicles for all categories I to V at the stage of the application in order to improve this procedure, fourthly, to delegate to the Director of Transport Technical Supervision the tasks of the Minister of Infrastructure to grant derogations from the technical conditions of vehicles in order to speed up and improve administrative procedures in this respect. The draft deadline for the entry into force of the Act is 1 January 2022. The exception will be the regulations concerning the transmission by Transport Technical Supervision of information on vehicle authorisations granted for derogation from the technical conditions of vehicles. Those provisions shall apply from the date of implementation by the Minister responsible for the computerisation of technical solutions for the transmission of that information. The deadline for implementing the solutions will be set in the Communication at least 3 months before their implementation. ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-13-sejm-39-1.u275	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! From general to detail. With regard to the subject matter of the amendment of the law - Road traffic law, this amendment responds to very dynamically changing conditions which are related to road transport, traffic. It's the order of things. We need to respond, we need to adapt our laws to these issues. Hence many amendments, in various areas, in different areas. I would like to remind you that this law is a very comprehensive law, regulates road safety issues, regulates issues related to road traffic, and regulates issues related to other areas of transport. So that these amendments are quite often and they will often continue, because, as I say, we need to respond to changing realities and adapt the Polish law to the situation that is here and now, but also to respond to the future, we also need to anticipate certain things and put them into standards, into a legal framework to respond with the perspective of several - or a teenage girl. With regard to the number of derogations that are currently issued by the Ministry of Infrastructure, this is about 1400 derogations issued annually, so that this is a certain scope that is being carried out by the Minister of Infrastructure, of course by an office that supports the work of the Minister of Infrastructure. Hence the idea of transferring it to Transport Technical Supervision, where the body will be the Director of Transport Technical Supervision. Why not the county? Here I answer to Mr Sosnierz. In the county there are no such workers, there are no people in our opinion who would be able to assess in such a substantive and unambiguous manner which vehicle can be used safely by carriers and for which derogation can be granted and which vehicle should not be subject to such a derogation because of its potential risk. Please note that different types of derogations from the legal standards in terms of road infrastructure, as well as building infrastructure are issued by central authorities: in terms of road infrastructure, by the Ministry of Infrastructure, and in terms of cubic infrastructure, i.e. buildings, it is currently a resort which is responsible for the department: construction, that is, the Ministry of Development. The transfer of this to the local government level in our opinion would cause a very big mess, chaos and such uncertainty in the counties themselves as to what to do with this matter. The districts or district districts have in their competence the supervision of vehicle control stations. You can also read from the reports of the Supreme Audit Chamber that there are some shortcomings here that there are some shortcomings here. Besides, which is actually good practice, in many cases old age eats the Transport Technical Controller by commissioning him with tasks related to the control of vehicle control stations. So you can see that it's better if it stays in the hands of professionals. Regardless of what office it will be, whether the minister's office or the authority, Transport Technical Supervision, it must remain in the hands of professionals. With regard to the use of cameras by local government units, we have had this example in the past. Supreme Audit Chamber In 2011 and 2013 it also pointed out that in the hands of the municipalities, the cameras were only supposed to repair these budgets. local government units. I admit we've changed that. Now also the speed cameras, and the staggering speed measurements are at the hands of the GITD, the chief road transport inspector. I think it's the best way to take care of safety on the one hand, and on the other hand, not to allow undercover, under the cloak of car safety to be repaired by local government budgets. Thank you very much.

However, the increase in these limits and other actions still do not meet the growing needs of patients for health care, and the effects are evident after years. At the same time, the Republic of Poland remains a country which constitutes an attractive market for the acquisition of highly qualified medical personnel, while losing it. On average, it is estimated that on average, about 900 medical graduates educated in Poland without specialization receive recognition of professional qualifications in the European Union or Norway. After all, graduates also go to other countries. Leaving a qualified doctor results in restricting access to medical services in the country. It's not. also of course good for patients who expect high standards of medical care and access to high-class specialists. Designed solutions are to meet these expectations. An important proposal is to make the conditions for universities to obtain permission to establish medical and dental studies, which will translate into an increase in both the number of universities conducting studies in these fields, as well as the number of students and graduates who, once they have completed their studies and specialized themselves, will feed on the ranks of Polish medical staff. The priority will, of course, be to ensure a sufficiently high level of education, while maintaining the conditions and quality of education, and this will be limited only to universities at academic level. Another proposal is the support from the state budget of those who undertake and receive paid studies in Polish for medical purposes with the obligation to work them later in entities which provide health services financed by public funds, i.e. the so-called. Loyalty commitment. In addition to the financial support of students taking up and taking up these studies, the proposed solution will help to reduce the phenomenon of emigration of Polish university graduates who are doctors and increase the number of specialist doctors, in particular in the areas of medicine considered as priority. In addition, the introduction of this support will be an opportunity for gifted persons to gain a doctor's profession, who, despite good results on the maturity exam, did not get into a fixed-mode medical course and which, among other things, did not get into a full-time medical course. because of the lack of sufficient financial resources, they could not undertake these studies for payment. If such students benefit from a medical degree loan, they will be able to withdraw the loan completely, provided that the doctor fulfils the following cumulative conditions: the obligation to study after completion for a period of not less than 10 years from the date of completion of their studies and the obligation to obtain a specialist title during that period considered as a priority on the day on which the doctor starts specialization training. Other important changes in the bill include: differentiation of the period of student benefits depending on the duration of the studies, as well as clarification of how this period is to be determined, making the student loan system more attractive for creditors, improving the process of verification of student and doctoral status during student credit and medical college loans, based on the Integrated Information System on Higher Education and Science, i.e. the so-called. POL-on. The objectives and proposed solutions outlined in the bill are considered to be good for the health and patient protection system, leading in the perspective of raising standards of medical care and improving access to high-quality specialists. Therefore, the Law and Justice Club will support the proposed project. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

These are the facilitations and these are the systemic changes that are also intended to redirect a stream of patients from the doctor level to the levels of other medical professions. Another facilitation today is, for example, facilitation for foreigners, I do not want to say controversial, but perhaps actually difficult to understand, especially by the environment of the General Medical Chamber. However, it must be said that they have the first results. That's $1,000. applications submitted by doctors, mainly from Ukraine. Nearly 700 have already been investigated. These are real actions that support the Polish health system today. Mr Szulowski - perhaps a few specific questions - asked what estimates we have today about these changes in the extension of the number of doctors' universities. These estimates are subject to a fairly high risk of error, so it is difficult to give a precise answer. The process of making it easier, i.e. creating some simplified formal criteria, those universities being universities that aspire to training doctors, is just a small step. There are still a lot of steps to be taken on the university side, such as going through accreditation procedures, going through a multi-level process of obtaining the approval of the Ministry of Science, the Ministry of Health, the Polish accreditation agency. These are processes that, unfortunately, often take years. Even today we see universities that have failed to start their medical course in spite of their efforts for several years. Mr Maksimowicz asked about quality control. She's gonna be the same as today. The fact that at the formal level we allow a larger group of universities, especially universities... I'm going to mention some examples of individuals who can aspire to this. That's for example. Maria Curie-Skłodowska University in Lublin, University of Natural Sciences and Humanities in Siedlce, Opole University of Technology. Today we identify a dozen universities that can aspire to this because they're training in medical professions. Today they educate physiotherapists, rescuers, nurses, which makes it a little easier for them to enter the level of doctor training. I say a little, because these accreditation standards are still exactly the same as the standards of any medical school that is teaching in medicine today. I remind you, there are 22 such universities. If you are asking for further action and what the Minister of Health has done, I answer that he has just extended this access as well. 5, 6 years ago, such universities of doctors were in Poland 15. I'm not talking about nurses who have increased this number by 50% over the last few years. And for example, significant expenditures on medical simulation centers in these universities have been incurred in recent years. In conclusion, accreditation standards remain the same. This path is very difficult. I agree with Professor Maximowicz that probably few universities will lean on this, and even if they bend over, they have to think really well, because the training of doctors is prestigious, it is indeed a distinction, but it is also expensive, the costs of investments that need to be borne are large. I believe that thanks to this law we will increase this number, but it will not be such a revolution that will make 20 additional universities start training doctors. Finally, I will answer one more question. Mrs Filiks spoke about a loan of PLN 0.5 million which young people would have to pay back and asked why not study free of charge. I've already told you that we've increased the admission limits for graduating free of charge by 50%. This potential of medical universities, universities subordinate to the Minister of Health or supervised by him ends as well. Today, the rectors of these universities are commenting on the fact that their teaching potential, the clinical potential, is slowly ending and these limits, even if we find sources of funding for them, these universities are not able to increase as actively as they have increased in the last few years. This, on the other hand, is not a 0.5 million credit, Madame. That's 180 grand. Or about 200,000. a loan that really such a young man can choose for, but on the other hand it is not a compulsion, it is not a obligation, it is an optional possibility to use such a loan instead of financing these studies with own funds. To be honest, today I probably find it harder to answer the question why such support is given only to doctors, and not to them. IT, economists, physicists, mathematicians, etc. I would probably have a problem with this question, but as regards the question why the State allows people who have not been able to study free of charge to borrow a loan which, moreover, if a person works 10 years in a public-funded health system in Poland, can be decommitted, I think it is only a benefit for these people. I see absolutely nothing wrong in this, and if for us, for the health system is an additional value, that these people will stay in Poland and work in the public system, I think that it will gain first and foremost the patient and gain the system, and they will also gain those people, because they will have a great determination that this loan will actually be surrendered to them. I also think, in my final opinion, that talking about how many people have left, how many are leaving, how much will be leaving, is probably such a game of statistics, but if we are already playing with statistics, then one thing is important: the number of certificates relating to trips that are made by young people in Poland since 2017, because such statistics in a quick manner I found, decreased by 50%. This shows that the desire to leave, the desire for labour migration is significantly less. However, there is still one, so the solutions contained in this law are also intended to limit it to even smaller levels and I believe that they will limit it. This law was well adopted by the academic community, by a conference of rectors of medical universities, well received by students. Mrs Cicholska spoke about meeting with certain people. Yes, in fact, we already have a whole bunch of questions from our parents in the ministry, from young people about whether this law is already coming into force or whether they can count on this loan. So we are convinced that this law is a good solution, that it will be adopted well and that many students will benefit from it, and thus we will extend the admission limits to medical studies even more. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-13-sejm-39-1.u369	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present my position on behalf of the Civic Coalition Club against the parliamentary draft law amending the Law on the Protection of Agricultural and Forest Lands, prints no. 1567 and 1591. The Civic Coalition Club is in favour of rejecting this project in its entirety. However, this is not due to criticism of the very idea of facilitating conditions for increasing employment and innovation in rural areas. We are in favour of making changes to make it easier to take up activities in rural areas. The proposal to reject this project is justified on formal grounds and, above all, by the poor quality of the project itself, which is to amend the Law of 3 February 1995 on the conservation of agricultural and forestry land. It is about what has already sounded at the committee's meeting: the biggest drawback of this project is its preparation in parliamentary rather than government work, which assumes a different mode of preparation of laws. The proposal is inconsistent and the proposed amendments contain elementary legislative errors. The content of these amendments shows how quickly and recklessly the draft law was drawn up, which is, after all, a very important issue: the protection of agricultural and forestry land and the introduction of exceptions to their protection. Worries must be raised as to whether this legislative idea will not turn into an instrument of dismantling and legal chaos of the existing system. The aim of the project is to simplify the taking-up or development by farmers, households, spouses of farmers of non-agricultural activities on agricultural land, in particular on land of Classes I, II and III, using the resources of the farm, by exempting from the need to use these land for non-agricultural purposes and by levying charges related to the exclusion of land from agricultural production in the event of non-agricultural activities. At this point I must quote the position of the Lower Silesian Agricultural Chamber addressed to the National Council of Agricultural Chambers. In fact, the aim of the legislator is to use agricultural land for non-agricultural activities, in particular under housing, without any restrictions or controls. The proposed solutions will remove farmers from arable land, increase agricultural land prices and thus contribute to the soon end of Polish, independent and family agriculture. More doubts. Does the very idea of exemption from the levy on land excluded from agricultural production make it unnecessary to initiate an exemption procedure in the form of a decision to exclude land from agricultural production? Despite the justification given by the text of the draft law itself, it does not appear that the obligation to obtain a decision on the exemption of land will not be necessary. And that's what's causing it, according to the article. Article 28 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 penalties in connection with the exclusion of incompatible with the provisions of the Act. The text of Amendment 2 to the project is also incomprehensible, and concerns the institution itself to notify the old lady of its intention to carry out its activities within 30 days before it is planned to take place. The explanatory memorandum states that 30 days of old age will allow a thorough examination of the notification in order to meet certain requirements. Is this notification a request or just a notification? Or does it play a common role in the form of a notification authorising the absence of a fee and at the same time an application for authorisation to exclude from agricultural production? From the content of the article. 12b. Point 1 (2) shows that the owner of land excluded from agricultural production must submit an appropriate application. And that makes it necessary to go through the whole procedure. The proposed regulation introduces two procedures: one for the owner in the form of an exclusion procedure, the other for the person who intends to carry out a non-agricultural activity in the housing sector. This is in the form of a notification. And here the Starost was not actually equipped with any control instrument concerning the notification, objection or decision on the content of the notification received, except for the right to impose a penalty in the absence of such notification. According to the draft, the consequences of non-compliance with the obligation to continue running the holding by the owner will be directly affected by the person who has entered into non-agricultural activities. However, there may be various cases of chance which will prevent the continuation of the holding, e.g. sickness or death. This is just an example of the inaccuracies of this regulation, which will introduce additional confusion into the already difficult bill. Yet the project is to introduce exceptions to the land protection system. Question: "Will regulation make the goal easier, or will it make the process more difficult by introducing additional legal confusion?" The trend of governing fashion is to punish as many Poles as possible, and we all know that the problem we are talking about does not exist only in the sphere of the future, but includes a huge number of cases where such activity in rural areas is already undertaken and conducted. The regulation should therefore also include provisions to correct the current situation. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Marshal, I'm almost done. High Izbo! This project is another document written on the knee with improvised amendments, which conflicts with the principle of careful legislation, with the vision of the legislator opting for solutions that are clear and not confusing to the legal system. I say this primarily in the interests of those whose regulation is dedicated. To sum up, I bring on behalf of the Parliamentary Club the Civic Coalition - Civic Platform, Modern, Polish Initiative, Greens about rejecting the project at second reading. And I would like to ask the Prime Minister to work towards a fair and clear proposal to amend the law on the protection of agricultural and forestry land. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-13-sejm-39-1.u401	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the report of the Committee on Culture and Media about the draft resolution on the establishment of the year 2022 by Maria Konopnicka. The Committee on Culture and Media, after first reading and considering this draft resolution at its meeting on 17 September 2021, invites the High Sejm to adopt a draft resolution. Let me add that the work on this project took place in a very good atmosphere. We extended this draft resolution by a whole paragraph concerning the work of Maria Konopnicka and the project was adopted unanimously by 19 votes in favour. I will read the text adopted at the meeting: In May 2022 the 180th anniversary of the birth of Maria Konopnicka - Polish writer, poet, translator and journalist was celebrated. On this occasion the Sejm of the Republic of Poland decides to honor one of the most outstanding writers in the history of Polish literature, the creator of 'Roty'. Maria Konopnicka, from the House of Wasiłowska, was born on 23 May 1842 in Suwałki, as a daughter of a lawyer, attorney of the Attorney General and patron of the Civil Court in Kalisz. Her mother died when Maria was 12. The father's upbringing, in an atmosphere of patriotism and Christian tradition, had a great impact on the writer. In the years 1855-1856 she studied on the salary of the sacramental sisters in Warsaw, where she met Eliza Pawłowska - later Narzeszkowa. In 1862, she married Jarosław Konopnicki, with whom she had eight children and lived in Bronów and then in Gusin. In 1876, after leaving her husband, she moved with her children to Warsaw, where she published, did homework and participated in patriotic and social activities. Around 1886, she met Maria Dulębianka, with whom she spent many years - living together, traveling and inspiring to act. Since 1890, after her children's emancipation, she began to travel around Europe a lot and visited, among others. Italy, Austria, Germany, France, Switzerland and the Czech Republic. Maria Konopnicka died on October 8, 1910 in Lviv. The funeral, attended by about 50,000 people, was a great patriotic manifestation. Maria Konopnicka made her debut in the journal 'Kaliszanin' in 1870 using the pseudonym 'Marko', but her proper poetic start was the cycle 'In the Mountains' from 1876 - favorably reviewed by Henryk Sienkiewicz. In 1881, she released the first volume of poetry. She edited the magazine 'Świt', collaborated with the weekly 'Iluszcz', many magazines, publications and Polish companies of three partitions and on the outskirts. She became famous for her novelist prose and children's works and was involved in literary criticism, among others. in 'Kurier Warszawski'. She was the most outstanding representative of folk musical styling, using poetic language derived from historical and fairy tales, inspired by the beauty of her native nature. She created lyrics and songs about patriotic and social themes and poetic pictures. She made a breakthrough in Polish literature for children, raising this field of writing to the heights of art. By means of literary works, she protested against anti-Polish policy of conquerors and social injustice. She co-organised a protest by the world public against the German repression of Polish children in Września between 1901 and 1902 and expropriation laws. She fought for women's rights and helped the Russian authorities imprisoned for political reasons. In 1902, the 25th anniversary of Maria Konopnicka's literary work took place in Krakow and in Lviv, and one year later she received a gift from the nation of the Polish manor with a park in Żarnowiec near Krosn. She was an honorary member of the Society of the People's School and many associations and institutions operating on Polish lands, in Europe and the USA. In 1908, she published 'Rota' - a patriotic poem that was a protest against Germanization policy in the Prussian partition and played a significant role in the history of Poles. For the first time, for Felix Nowowiejski's music, it was performed on July 15, 1910 at the Grunwald Memorial in Krakow, which was funded by Ignacy Jan Paderewski - on the occasion of the 500th anniversary of the victory over the Teutonic Order. In 1926 the authorities of the independent Republic considered 'Rota' as a national anthem. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland, appreciating the literary achievements of Maria Konopnicka and her contribution to the patriotic activity, establishes the year 2022 as the Year of Maria Konopnicka. Thank you. [[Applause]]

Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Allow me, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Polish Coalition - Polish People's Party, the Union of European Democrats, the Conservatives, to present the position of the club on the establishment of the year 2022 of Maria Konopnicka. The positivists tried to introduce their views into beautiful literature, especially novels. In those days, in times of change, Maria Konopnicka became a writer. In 1881, the first series of poetic works gathered in two collections of Poets and Images was published, very favorably evaluated by Józef Kraszewski. He saw in Konopnicki's works a new phenomenon that has not yet been seen in Polish literature. In these collections of poetry, as in later works, Konopnicka addressed topics of slavery, social clashes, problems of everyday life of the poorest. For us folks, the Rota written by Maria Konopnicka in 1908 is of particular importance. Complemented with music by Felix Nowowiejski has become not only an extremely important patriotic song, but also an anthem of folk movement. Singing during the patriotic celebrations was a testimony to the spirit of the struggle for independence. Today also Rota is proudly sung by Poles. Maria Konopnicka played an extremely important role in the development of children's literature, as a poet she speaks strongly to her small readers. She recognized the right of a child to a carefree childhood. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The children's world that she created was bright and joyful. Even if evil appeared in the way of her children's heroes, by the help of good people or supernatural powers they were defeated, and by identifying with a literary hero, a small reader gained faith in his own abilities and a happy solution to failures. Thank you. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Coalition Poland, I ask the High House to adopt a resolution establishing the year 2022 of Maria Konopnicka. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-13-sejm-39-1.u427	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo!
