It remains to me to thank applicants for this project, this commemoration and to express the satisfaction that there will be a chance to remind, as well as to commemorate and disseminate knowledge in Poland and the world about one of the most extraordinary Poles of the 20th century, about the person who touched anything, turned it into gold. Let me read the text of this resolution. This is actually the only opportunity for this text to sound in the plenary room of the Polish Parliament. The resolution of the Polish Sejm on the establishment of the year 2022 by Wanda Rutkiewicz. In 2022, thirty years have passed since Wanda Rutkiewicz's death. On the 30th anniversary of the death of Wanda Rutkiewicz, the Sejm of the Republic of Poland pays tribute to one of the world's greatest Himalaists. Wanda Rutkiewicz, from the house of Błaszkiewicz, was born on 4 February 1943 in Płungian, Lithuania. After the end of the war, she was raised in Wroclaw. She received a degree in electronics engineer at the University of Technology. After moving to Warsaw, she became connected scientifically with the Institute of Mathematical Machines - a centre that forms the foundations of Polish computer science. She specialized in computer memory work. In the 1980s, it belonged to 'Solidarity', and it was important for it to be active in the democratic opposition. Since she was a child, she showed interest in sports, and her love for the mountains aroused an expedition to the Janowicki Reddaw band during her studies. Then she experienced what she described in the book 'On the One Line', unusual feelings: 'Fear and joy of defeating it, focus and determination, freedom from gravity, extremely strong contact with nature, with a piece of rock that was my support'. After completing the Taternite course, she bravely invaded the men's world of climbers, often hearing words that 'babes are not suitable for him because they are weaker'. She was overcoming the barriers, she was the forerunner of women's mountain ranges, because she was not willing to let talented mountain climbers 'be only broochs in men's expeditions'. She impressed with strength, physical fitness and extraordinary intelligence. Andrzej Zawada, a prominent Himalayan, admitted years later: 'None of us realized that this calm, somewhat from another era, girl has an iron character, enormous willpower and can be extremely disciplined in action'. In the mountains she felt freedom, as her words remind her: 'And we on top, through effort and fatigue cleansed and free, though for a moment become a part of nature'. The mountains treated as an area of extraordinary rivalry, they wanted victory over them. She won fights in the Tatras, the Alps, the Andes, Pamir, the Hindus, the Himalayas, often the first women's transitions. On October 16, 1978, she won Mount Everest. She was the first person from Poland and the third woman in the world to stand on the highest peak of the Earth. On the same day, Charles Wojtyla became Pope. A year later, during his visit to Poland, John Paul II said to Wanda Rutkiewicz: 'Good God so wanted that on the same day we entered so high'. She was the first woman in the world to stand on the hardest to reach the K2 summit. Rivalry with the mountains was associated with a huge risk, without which she could not live. Mountains were her world, they allowed for exceptional intellectual and aesthetic sensations, because climbing recognized her as a work of art. Rutkiewicz created unique works. She gained fame, her exploits were known all over the world. The fight to win the eight-thousands called 'Caravana to Dream', because she believed that it was possible to 'force something that seems to be realized only in dreams'. Her fantastic sports achievements aroused admiration, and she also enchanted with grace, charm and an extraordinary gift of telling stories about the mountains. She didn't come back from the 9th millennium expedition. She disappeared on May 12, 1992 on her way to Kanchendzonga. Since then, Wanda Rutkiewicz's fate has not been known, her body has not been found. 'Life tastes best when you can lose it' - she kept repeating. After a few years, the Warsaw court declared the date of her death on 13 May 1992. Wanda Rutkiewicz has won many awards and awards. Indeed, no one like Polka fought to break the male hegemony in world mountaineering and the proper role of women in it. 'Woman of striking beauty and energy, the greatest mountain climber of her epoch' - are the words of an expert on Himalaism, a well-known English journalist Richard Cowper. Leszek Cichy - the first conqueror of Mount Everest in winter, calls Wanda Rutkiewicz the great personality of world Himalaism. And no one will even think to write a separate sentence for such opinions. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland announces the year 2022 with the Year of Wanda Rutkiewicz. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-13-sejm-39-1.u455	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, On behalf of the Committee on Culture and Media, I have the honour to present to you the report of the Committee on Culture and Media on the draft resolution on the establishment of 2022 of Józef Rufin Wybicki, prints 1138 and 1272. I would like to thank all of you working in the Committee on Culture from this point of view for working together on this project and adopting this resolution unanimously, in a very creative and compatible atmosphere. The Committee on Culture addressed these projects at its meeting on 28 September this year. A common text of the resolution has been drawn up, which I have the honour of presenting to you. The resolution of the Polish Sejm on the establishment of the year 2022 by the Year of Józef Wybicki. Józef Rufin Wybicki was born on 29 September 1747 in Bedomina, Kaszubach, in the family of the medieval Pomeranian nobility. He began his political career by taking part in the royal election of Stanisław August Poniatowski in 1764. His protests in 1768 during the repninist sejm, which the contemporaries considered to be one of the first activities of the Bar Confederation to which Wybicki joined, went down to history. Józef Wybicki has repeatedly been a Member of the Sejm, but he has not been elected as the most important Grand Sejm sitting in the years 1788-1792. Despite this, he was involved in the work on the implementation of the Constitution on 3 May as a representative of the state of the bourgeois state. After the Russian intervention and the victory, Tarkowiczan took part in secret preparations for the explosion of Kościuszko Insultation. After the fall of the insurrection he went to Paris, where thanks to his efforts he was able to obtain permission to create a Polish army alongside France. In July 1797, in Reggio Emilia, Italy, he wrote 'The Song of the Polish Legions in Italy', which has been a Polish national anthem since 1927. It was Józef Wybicki from Gen. Jan Henryk Dąbrowski who wrote a message to Poles referring to Napoleon's words and created the foundations of the Duchy of Warsaw. In this substitute of free Polish Wybicki became a voivode and a senator. Józef Wybicki died on March 10, 1822. In 2022 it will be 200 years since his death, 275 years since his birth and 225 years since the founding of the 'Polish Legions in Italy'. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland, convinced of the unique importance of the achievements of Józef Wybicki, pays tribute to his achievements, establishes the year 2022 as the Year of Józef Wybicki. On behalf of the Committee on Culture and Media, I propose that the High Sejm adopt the attached draft resolution. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-13-sejm-39-1.u461	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! And these were times of extreme interest, as well as interesting was Józef Wybicki himself, one of the most outstanding representatives of Polish enlightenment. Wybicki was a writer, politician, author of patriotic letters read during Thursday lunches at King Stanisław Augustus, work on economic reforms and the marketing of paper money. He was also the author of a call to Poles living in the Prussian partition, suggesting that Napoleon will resurrect Poland if Poles prove worthy of being a nation, and which resolution contributed to the outbreak of the victorious Greater Poland Uprising. Wybicki also co-created the foundations of the Duchy of Warsaw, where he became a voivode and a senator. Between 1815 and 1821 he was the President of the Supreme Court of the Kingdom of Poland. He died in 1922. His ashes lie in the Crypt of the Decent Great Poles in the Church of St. Wojciech in Poznań. Józef Wybicki was a bachelor of the Order of the White Eagle, the Order of St. Stanisława and the Legion of Honor, and now in recognition of merit will be the patron of 2022, which will contribute to the greater fame of his name and activity, which has served Poland so well. Therefore, the Left Club proposes that a resolution be adopted by acclamation. Thank you for your attention.

Maria Grzegorzewska in Warsaw, an institution founded by one of the most outstanding Polish teachers - Prof. Maria Grzegorzewska. On this occasion the Sejm of the Republic of Poland pays tribute to the outstanding creator of special pedagogy in Poland. Maria Grzegorzewska, nicknamed occupation 'Narcisza', outstanding scientist, special teacher, Tyflopedagog, Tyflopsychologist, social activist - was born on 18 April 1888 in the village of Wołucza near Rawa Mazowiecka, died in Warsaw on 7 May 1967. The motto of her life and life of the next generations of special educators, specialists working for people with disabilities, made a statement: 'There is no cripple - there is a man'. Maria Grzegorzewska emphasized that everyone has an archimedes point, thanks to which one can discover the power in man and save the fullness of his humanity. Its message is still up to date: 'Beautiful words they only cause harm if they are not supported by action, even by an attempt to act'. She herself was a proponent of the idea of combining theory with practice, penetrating the past into the future. Her works are an inspiration for further researchers and creators of academic special pedagogy and educational, therapeutic and rehabilitation practice. Special value was given to the work of a teacher teacher, whom she saw as a man rich internally, charismatic and empathetic. Sejm of the Republic of Poland on the 100th anniversary of the establishment of the Academy of Special Pedagogics. ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-13-sejm-39-1.u481	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Maria Grzegorzewska - educator in the service of disabled children, model and inspiration for many generations of teachers. She was the first in Poland to undertake systematic research on the pedagogy of people with disabilities in the 20th anniversary of the interwar period. She professionally addressed the issues of special pedagogy, especially Tyflopedagogy, i.e. education of blind, visually impaired, but also deaf people. She also addressed the issue of teacher education. She paid particular attention to the compensatory and remedial functions of teaching work with a disabled child. It created the theoretical and organizational basis of the special education system in Poland. We owe it to the creation in 1922. The National Institute of Special Pedagogics, now known as the Academy of Special Pedagogics. Maria Grzegorzewska in Warsaw. It is also due to the exhibition of the teacher's charism. She said, "The better the teacher will be, the better the world and the life of every man." The teacher, according to her concept, primarily interacts with his own personality. For this effect to be positive, the basic features of his personality were, among others, responsible. Love, kindness and care. During the occupation, she was also faithful to her values. She participated in the underground educational activity, organized aid for persecuted Jews, and during the Warsaw Uprising she was a member of the health patrol on Ochota. In the post-war years she was a professor at the University of Warsaw and a director of the first university cathedral of special pedagogy in Poland. In recognition of Maria Grzegorzewska's pioneering, outstanding contribution to the development of Polish pedagogy, the special club Law and Justice positively views the draft resolution of the Polish Sejm on the establishment of the year 2022 by the Year of Maria Grzegorzewska. High Izbo! Maria Grzegorzewska, special teacher, Tyflopsychologist, Tyflopedagog, conducted secret teaching during the occupation and participated in the Warsaw Uprising as a paramedic. She was the first in Poland to undertake systematic research on issues of disabled pedagogy, and created its basis. She specially cared for the psychology and care of the blind. Its merit is the development of theoretical assumptions of special pedagogy as well as methodology. She conducted extensive research and research at a global level. In direct contact with various physical and mental disabilities, special education and pedagogy have become her passion. In writing, the social introduction of cripple people of all kinds as useful workers and the compensation of the social harm of these individuals, at the same time defined their purpose in life. She treated pedagogy as an integral science related to educational practice. She believed that teaching must serve the current educational reality, and that the teacher should have moral qualifications in addition to expertise. At the same time, she emphasized the importance of the child's activities, the role of the environment, family, peer group or after-school education. By editing the magazine Special School, it enabled teachers of special schools to learn the latest achievements of special pedagogy. She stressed the importance of working methods with children with different developmental deficiencies, prevention, the removal of their defects, as well as recognition. She has developed the original method of teaching, which is called today's method of working centers, which is now widely used in special education. According to the assumption, this method is not limited to teaching, but includes the shape of the educational problems of children with disabilities. She considered the work related to teacher education to be the most important achievement. She felt that the success of the educational process depended primarily on the teacher. In Lists to a young teacher, he writes: "Every man must pass through the school. The better the teacher, the better the world and the life of every man. The motive that dominated her activities, and the motto of life was the sentence: She fought for the full right to study, to work, and to respect the handicapped. She argued that disability does not diminish the value and dignity of man (the Bell), which in today's world plays an important role in shaping behaviour in society. For all these reasons, High Izbo, the Parliamentary Circle Poland 2050 supports the establishment of the year 2022 of Maria Grzegorzewska and encourages the entire House to support this proposal. Thank you very much.

The origin of the formation of the NRA should be briefly recalled here. In response to the repression of power after the communist dictatorship announced the rise in food prices on the streets, thousands of protesters appeared. There were strikes in hundreds of workplaces across Poland. Then the power bowed and the raises were cancelled. However, she took revenge by using hate propaganda and brutally stalking the workers of several hundred workplaces, including Ursus, Radom and Płock. Participants of protests met torture and prison sentences, and Fr. Roman Kotlarz supporting the prayers of strikers was tortured to death. There was a spontaneous citizen movement initiated by Scouts 1. The Warsaw Scouts Team, who, together with the youth of academic ministry, reached families persecuted with material aid, legal and financial support agreed with lawyers focused around mec. Jan Olszewski. Thanks to their determination on 23 September 1976, the Committee for the Defence of Workers was born, to which the veterans fought for independence of Poland, priests, writers and scientists, scouting young people educated on the legend of the Old Private and the Polish leaders and participants of the protests of March 1968. They were people with different ideal views, but together claiming civil rights and human freedom, as well as the initiators of the struggle for independence of their homeland. Changes in the draft resolution are to commemorate the victims and heroes who helped in those difficult times, showing great courage. This resolution is important because of the dedication to those who formed the NRA and the nation of historical truth. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The main objective of the Committee was to provide legal and financial assistance to persecuted workers and their families. KOR was the first open opposition organization in the Polish People's Republic. The KOR played an important role during the strike in August 1980 and contributed to the legalization of the first behind the iron curtain of free trade unions. The most important success of the NRA was to break the social apathy and fear of communist dictatorship. According to historians, the KOR gave birth to other anti-communist opposition initiatives and organisations in the times of the Polish People's Republic. Today for many Poles - both for those who remember the communist reality and for the generation born in the Third Republic - the KOR is a beautiful card of history of Polish aspirations for independence and liberation from the domination of the Soviet Union. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland hereby decides to express its highest gratitude to the creators and activists of the Committee for the Defence of Workers and to all Poles who have served Poland well in support of the NRA. ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-13-sejm-39-1.u645	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! 100th anniversary of the Dalin Myślenice Sports Club. Sports in Myślenice have a long tradition. Its beginning dates back to the 1920s. In the spring of 1921 in the apartment of the Walchers State at ul. A group of sports enthusiasts met chasing 5, among them. Stanisław Jankiewicz, Gustaw Grochal, Aleksander Boryczko, Stanisław Pazgan, the Holuj brothers and the Walcher brothers. The aim of the meeting was to set up a sports club that would enable young people and all interested in active sports, in particular football, in an organized manner. The founders of the newly formed club, following in their actions the ancient motto of the healthy body of a healthy spirit, gave the club the name Dyskos, also derived from ancient times. The established club was deprived of any sports facilities. Even meetings of activists were held in the apartments of its members. Initially, the surrounding meadows of the Raba in Myślenice owned by the Lubomirski manor were used for the competition. In 1922 the club changed its name to KS Uklejna and played the first official football match in Myślenice. The opponent was the KS Raba team from Dobczyce. Unfortunately, after a while, the Raba River took out the football club. As a result of many floods, it was completely absorbed by the river's current. Unscathed by this fact, sports enthusiasts almost immediately began their efforts to create a new field and already in 1924 under the agreement with the owners of the goods of the Duke of Lubomirski began to mark and build a new field. Thanks to the involvement of football players and activists, the first football field was created in Myślenice, in accordance with the rules. The field enjoyed great success among thinkers and was a place for organizing not only sports events, but also cultural events. In 1925 KS Uklejna was merged with the Gymnastic Society of the Falcon. The newly formed club adopted the name Falcon-Podhale. Connecting with a strong gymnast in the city has brought great benefits to the club. In August 1939, the KS Sokół-Podhale Myślenice football team achieved the greatest success in the pre-war history of the club, winning during the Kalwaria Zebrzydowska Cup of Podhale. The tragic year 1939 interrupted the club's activities. However, despite the ban on mass sports, the club's players secretly met over Raba to practice and train. The greatest activity was achieved in 1943. The end of any sports activity was only 1944. This had to do with numerous arrests among club activists in connection with their contacts with the Home Army guerrilla. The club was resumed shortly after the crossing of the front line. Already on February 11, 1945, in the house of the State of Pitals at ul. Bema 2 in Myślenice held a meeting of thinkers and sports enthusiasts. Returning to the stage of the Thinking Sports club needed a new name. The proposals were dominated by local geographical names such as Raba, Uklejna or Chełm, but ultimately the name Dalin was chosen. The preparations for registration of the sports club began and the date of the general meeting, which took place on 15 March 1945, was set in the Sokoła building acquired by the club. It adopted the statute of the Dalin Sports Society and selected a new board. In the same year, the football section was reactivated, which staged two teams in the competition organized by the Kraków District Football Association, as well as the gymnastics and athletic sections. In the following years, the club developed, and further sections were created. In their ranks thinking, and not only they, could develop and continue to develop their sports interests and passions, hitting Polish and foreign sports arenas from Dalin to Polish and foreign sports arenas, giving us fans a lot of satisfaction. The most dynamic and popular sections in which players achieved the greatest success can include football, volleyball and wrestling. Ladies and gentlemen! As you can see, sport has always been part of our thinking identity. Activators and players (The Bell), all those who joined the history of the club, were and still form part of a beautiful thoughtful history KS Dalin club, but also our city and region. All the players, coaches and activists who contributed to the establishment and development of the club, and thus to the promotion of Myślenice and a healthy lifestyle, I give my warm thanks. Thank you very much. I also thank the fans who supported the club throughout its 100-year tradition. I hope there's gonna be a lot more... Thank you very much, sir. ...other beautiful cards of our common sports history. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-13-sejm-39-1.u647	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Another city of Piotrkowska land, which I wanted to introduce today, is the Sulejów. Today's Sulejów is a small town, which has laid out picturesquely on both sides of the beautiful Pilica. Boricing with many problems today, the town was once a place where many resorts functioned. They belonged primarily to Piotrków's workplaces and spent a lot of piotrkowians working in industrial piers such as PIOMA, Hortensia Glassworks and Kara Glassworks. The contemporary social facilities with the legendary Sulejovska Polanka allowed to spend time in a pleasant way. The Sulejów are not only well-known resorts in the area, which did not withstand the competition in modern tourism after 1990 and disappear from the urban landscape. The town grew up nearby, in the shadow of one of the largest Cistercian abbeys on Polish lands. The origins of Sulejov are most likely related to the customs chamber existing here at the crossing through Pilica. The Cistercians of Morimond, who were brought in in 1176, settled in a place today called the Subclassical. They certainly had an impact on the city's acquisition of urban rights in the middle of the 13th century. To this day you can watch almost a full monastery band with a beautiful Gothic Opack and Romanesque tower or, as some people say, early Gothic church under St. Tomasz Cantaurski and the hotel, which is built next to the church. The body of this building was severely damaged during the martial law, during the break-up of the dams on the nearby Pilica. For a very long time the temple was closed to the faithful, and Masses were held in the same church as the unique medieval hall, where the ceiling is based on only one column. Lying on one of Sulej's main shopping routes, he witnessed synods and reunions. It was through this city that one of the columns of Jagiełła's troops went to the great war with the Teutonic Order. After the Swedish Sulei flood, he fell. At the beginning of the 19th century, the Cistercian Order was erased and the monastery was destroyed. The town even lost its city rights, which it was not able to recover until 1927. Large deposits of limestone were found at Sulejov. A mine and numerous companies to burn lime were quickly organized. Just before the outbreak of World War I, 1,200 wagons of lime were sent from Sulejów. Regular railway communication between Piotrków and Sulejow was opened thanks to the small but efficient narrow-track railway. To this day, neither the narrow track railway nor even the remains of industrial history of the city have survived. During the Second World War, the Sulejów were destroyed in 80%, which is more than Wieluń, where the urban substance was destroyed in 75% and over 2,000. His people lost their lives. There were no military targets in Sulejów, but in the first days of September a massive bombing took place. I said that the municipality and the city of Sulejów are facing a lot of problems. The most important challenges in addition to the construction of sewers and roads are the improvement of the state of the water supply in the municipality and the construction of the S12 road. The road between Piotrków and Sulejov is sometimes called the road of death not only because of the intensity of traffic, but, of course, the numerous accidents in which people are simply dying and very heavy traffic. So finally, let's just build the S12 road as soon as possible. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-13-sejm-39-1.u653	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In the village Pękowiec in the municipality of Włoszczów there are remains of bunkers of guerrilla troops from the period of the Second World War. It was built in 1944 in the depths of the forest complex. The bunkers were mainly used as shelter during the last winter of war. About 60 and 70 soldiers were quartered in them. 74. Army Infantry Regiment. In the construction of five bunkers were involved members of Mieczysław Tarchalski's branch named Marcin and local foresters. Bunkers of size 4 by 4 m were partially dug into the ground to naturally reflect the shape of the hills. Four of the five bunkers were open to the soldiers. Three of them were residential and were equipped, among others. In the bunk bunks. The fourth bunker served as a training place that could accommodate 30 soldiers and in case of bad weather was a place to eat. Windows in all bunkers went outside the entire square and were so constructed that, if necessary, fire could be led from them to the attacking enemy. The fifth bunker contained a warehouse of weapons, ammunition and military equipment. Its location within 200 m was well masked. The ground floor was located about 3 m below ground level and had an atrium ending at the top with a box filled with ground in which the tree was planted. To get to that bunker, you had to know which tree to lift up and with it the box. Then there was a vertical hatch into the vestibule that climbed up the ladder. He was separated from the main room by a locked door. The bunker due to moisture and lack of proper ventilation required constant ventilation and cleaning of weapons. The location of the bunkers in the forest area and the lack of security for the soldiers. On 6 January 1945 in the guerrilla camp was hosted by the chief commander of the AK Gen. Leopold Okulicki nickname Bear. Until our time, the pits have been preserved, which reflect the distribution of bunkers from those years. Near the camp there is a monument, a memorial stone dedicated to General Okulicki, colonel. Bokszczanin, the fallen soldiers of the Major's battalion. Tarchalski's Marcina and the soldiers of 74. Army Infantry Regiment. Every year patriotic celebrations are held in Pęczowiec to commemorate soldiers fighting for independence. The lack of financial compensation for local government units and the obligations imposed on them are contrary to the provisions of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. .Art. 167. Revenue of local government units. 1. Local government units shall be provided with a share of public income according to their respective tasks. 2. The revenue of local government units is their own income and general subsidies and special subsidies from the State budget. 3. Sources of revenue of local government units are specified in the Act. 4. Changes in the tasks and competences of local government units follow with appropriate changes in the distribution of public revenue. I therefore call on the President of the Council of Ministers to take effective action, which will result in a system of compensation to supplement losses in local government's own income resulting from existing legislation harmful to local communities.

Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! 110 years ago, on September 29, 1911, Stanisław Jankowski was born in Warsaw - Polish architect, captain of Polish Army artillery, and also silent. During World War II, he was an intelligence officer of the National Army and a participant in the Warsaw Uprising - in 1944 he became a commander of the Platoon of Agaton in the Battalion of Fist. His father and brother were shot in Palmirach. Stanisław Jankowski was a personal aide of General Tadeusz Bora-Komorowski. After the war he worked as an architect at the Warsaw Development Planning Office and then abroad. He was an expert on the International Red Cross. Honored, among others. The Silver Cross of the Order of Virtuti Militari, the Cross of Valiant, the Cross of the Order of Rebirth of Poland. He died on 5 March 2002 in Warsaw. Hello and praise the heroes.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-13-sejm-39-1.u677	Dear Prime Minister, The Constitution of the Republic of Poland contains a catalogue of the rights of every person residing in Poland. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 57 We have guaranteed everyone freedom of organisation and participation in public gatherings, and in Article. 32 We have ensured that everyone, regardless of political views, is equal to the law and that public authority must treat everyone in the same way. The events which took place, inter alia, on 10 October 2021, show that in fact the Polish authorities and law enforcement authorities apply these principles in a selective manner. When the activists in Kraków Przedmieście organized a happening which was legally registered as a public assembly in the city office in the morning, the police used considerable force to prevent this happening from taking place, and all those who wanted to take part in this legal assembly did not have the opportunity to do so. Whether the reason for this was the fact that happening was about the action of the authorities and, in particular, about the Deputy Prime Minister for Foreign Affairs. Jarosław Kaczyński's security? Civil freedom protected by the Constitution in Article. 54 is the freedom of expression and dissemination of information, power cannot gag citizens when they express their views or ask questions that are not in its way. The law enforcement authorities, however, did not focus only on preventing a legal assembly of activists from being held. Some of them spent 35 hours in custody, and then by decision of the district attorney's office, undoubtedly politically determined, one of the activists received police supervision and a restraining order to approach the square where he had previously registered a legal public assembly. Among the alleged violations of the law were, among other things, the violation of the home mire, which concerns the entry into the public square of Józef Piłsudski in Warsaw. Such actions bring to mind the practice of the right past when the Security Service, together with the Civic Militia, tormented the citizens who were demonstrating against power. Events from Warsaw are not something isolated. An even more bizarre situation occurred in Krakow. During the demonstration under the regional office of your party, Law and Justice, mothers and children gathered to express their solidarity with mothers and children who were stuck on the Polish border with Belarus. During this convention, children aged 3 to 7 years with chalk made several drawings and inscriptions on the sidewalk. None of them were offensive, none of them violated good manners. However, police officers have decided to stop the assembly and the happening that took place during it. Little children were taken away from chalk and were banned from walking on painted drawings and inscriptions so that, as one of the officers said, they would not destroy evidence. Even a specialist criminal was called in to evaluate drawings and inscriptions. The course of these events seems absurd and sucked out of the finger, unfortunately it is true. Cops didn't even convince one of the three-year-olds that it was a chalk that was draining under the rain. The case ended by sending a call for an interrogation for the mothers gathered that day at the protest, there was only a lack of as absurd accusations as in Warsaw. Should this be the way the Polish Police and the Public Prosecutor's Office should work, which should be trusted by all citizens? It's a rhetorical question that everyone knows the answer to. However, the police show a very different attitude towards activists working for the ruling party. The best example of this is once again events from the centre of Warsaw. When 10 October 2021 at 3 p.m. 18 there was an assembly of citizens and citizens on the matter of staying Poland in the structures of the European Union and stopping Polexit, near activists gathered around Mr Bąkiewicz launched an audience whose aim was not to express opinions and opinions, but to play music to disturb the legally notified assembly. Moreover, Mr Bąkiewicz and his associates did their work not at the place where they reported their assembly, but a few hundred meters away. Did the police take appropriate action? Has she interrupted an illegal assembly, or has she stopped Mr. Bąkiewicz? None of this happened. The only action the police have taken is to set up a cordon around the sound system used to jamm a legal assembly. Does this mean the police are involved in current political events? Does her political supervision in the form of Minister Kaminski force certain actions to be taken and not to take them, if that is in line with his views? Should this be the case with the service that is supposed to protect the Constitution and the legal order in Poland? Does the Prime Minister support such action by law enforcement? Will the Prime Minister take action against the ministers of his government so that they can draw the right consequences for the subordinate services and institutions? Did you feel that the ministers of your government should respect the principles of the Constitution and make it happen in their institutions?
ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-13-sejm-39-1.u678	Statement on Medical Rescue Day. 13 October is an exceptional date, as the Medical Rescue Day has been celebrated every year since 2006. This day is a celebration of the Polish medical rescue, which was established by the Act of 8 September 2006 on State Medical Rescue. At this point, it is worth paying attention - and taking time - to the importance of the work of rescuers. Medical paramedics do hard, responsible and stressful work. Because of their hard work, they often risk their lives and health to reach out to the needy on time and help him. They are also the first to appear at the scenes of accidents. They are not strangers to intervention at times of disaster, sudden illness and first aid to those who need it. Only in 2019 - according to statistics - medical rescue teams helped 3.1 million people in Poland. As part of the 2019 PRM there were 1577 medical rescue teams: 369 specialist and 1208 basic. The assistance of the victims was also provided by 21 flight rescue teams and 237 hospital rescue units. I would like, through my statement, to express my greatest gratitude to all paramedics, doctors and system nurses, medical dispatchers and other people involved in the medical emergency system and to wish them the best of their holidays. I wish you all the best and, above all, the great satisfaction and joy of saving human life.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-10-13-sejm-39-1.u679	Reports from stakeholders in my parliamentary offices and media information show that in Poland there may be alleged irregularities in the mass export of stored fertilisers abroad. If the above were confirmed in the facts, this would be very important for the current situation on the fertiliser market, which is known to be difficult at present. If, in fact, there are infringements, given that the SSP is responsible for this area, it is important to safeguard the interests of Polish farmers and only then profit. The possible disposal of fertiliser stocks may result in a worsening of the difficult situation which has worsened since my interpellation in this regard. I also stressed the need to subsidize fertilisers and return to so-called. factory stores. Thank you for being included in the recently announced United Right for Agriculture programme. In view of the above, it should be pointed out that it appears appropriate for the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development to take action to control the operators involved in the production and storage of fertilisers. If, as a result of the inspection, there are requests for irregularities in the export of fertilisers abroad, the next appropriate step should be to block such processes.

On 7 and 8 June 2003, we went to referenda to vote on this matter. Over 77% Poles and Poles who voted on the question of consent to join the EU answered: yes. In Bielsko-Biała, 82% of the accession to the Community was supported voters and in the powiats: Bielski - 78% , Teschen - 83% , Pszczyński - 79% , lively - 80%. These results show that we have understood very well the prospect of ensuring our homeland's safety and, on the other hand, of obtaining a strong acceleration of economic growth. As a result, on 1 May 2004, we joined the European Union and became part of it. Today we can congratulate ourselves on this decision. Thanks to it, we are an important member of a unique club of European countries, which combines economic and political ties and a community of values. Because it is worth recalling that the European Union is not only politics, economy and money, but above all values such as solidarity, the rule of law, responsibility, honesty, tolerance, respect for work, economy. The Community of values, which also stems from the Christian roots of Europe, is an extremely strong binder. It is now time that we must defend this decision against the power that wants to put Poland outside the European Union. Before the authority, which for its own benefit decided to sacrifice the key interest of Poland. We owe it to our ancestors who fought for Poland free and independent. We owe it to the next generation of Poles and Poles whose future lies in our hands. ♪ If we let the power drop ♪ We will be remembered as those who have wasted one of the greatest opportunities in Poland's history. Let us do everything we can to win Poland's interests in this fight. Remember: The Union is all of us.
